PNG decoder conformance

Every decoder below was run over the same 798 small PNG files from difftest-png, each one built to test one rule of the PNG specification or one behaviour on which some decoder deviates. Run 2026-10-23T22:13:22+00:00 on Linux x86_64.

A test lists the outcomes it allows: pass, should (the specification says a decoder should do otherwise, e.g. recover a recoverable fault instead of rejecting the image) or must (a requirement is broken: wrong pixels, an invalid file accepted, a valid file rejected, a crash, a hang, a memory error). Warnings never count against a decoder. Running out of memory counts like a crash: a file should be rejected with an error, and a decoder that instead exhausts its host hands every program that opens untrusted images a denial of service. Pixels are compared as 16-bit RGBA with no colour management; colour under fully transparent pixels and the 8-bit reduction of 16-bit images are recorded as properties, not failures.

Two editions of the specification matter: the second (ISO/IEC 15948:2003) and the third (W3C, 2025). They contradict each other in one place: a palette index past the end of PLTE is an error in the second edition and must render opaque black in the third. On those tests a decoder passes if it does what either edition requires, and the summary says which edition it follows; mixed means it follows one edition on some of those tests and the other on the rest. Features the third edition only adds (APNG, cICP, eXIf) do not make a decoder mixed.

Not tested: animation frames after the default image, colour management (gAMA, cHRM, iCCP, sRGB, cICP, sBIT), encoders, performance. Passing every test means the suite is incomplete, not that the decoder is perfect.

Human disclaimer

The results likely contain errors, or decisions which can be argued with. Regardless, I did take greater-than-usual care to make this meaningful. The code is probably vibeslop, and I haven't read a single line. But, the decisions here came from several rounds of Matt Pocock's grill-me skill and at least four separate essays I wrote while trying to keep Claude Opus 5.5 on track, alongside several rounds of adversarial checking from GPT-6 Astra and from Claude Fable 5.1. I had to "push back" quite a lot to get past the AI's boneheaded decisions.

My conclusion: Parsing PNGs is a Minefield.

Section rfc1950-2
i_zlib-cinfo-0-long-distance
[rfc1950-2.2#window-exceeded] image data: a distance reaches further back than the window CINFO declares
rfc1950-2.2#window-exceeded: A distance reaches further back than the window CINFO declares (but within 32768). RFC 1950 gives no decompressor rule; zlib accepts it unless built with INFLATE_STRICT, libpng's sized window refuses it. Reference: decoded.
allowed: pixels: pass, reject: pass

i_deflate-empty-dynamic-block
